\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}z_{1}\text{d}z_{2}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptaz_{1}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptbz_{2}}z_{1}^{p_{1}}z_{2}^{p_{2}}\check{D}_{ab\,{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k},z_{1},z_{2})\,.

The seed integrals
\check{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 8.53581pt{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
are related to the hatted counterparts
\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 8.53581pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
through the relation,

\check{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 5.69054pt{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=-\!\left(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{-a\,-b\hskip 1.42262pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}^{*}p_{2}^{*}}\right)^{\!*}.

(73)

To verify this identity explicitly, we consider the choice od
(a,b)\!=\!(\ominus,\oplus)
, which means
\check{\mathcal{I}}_{\ominus\oplus\hskip 4.2679pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\!=\!-\!\left(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\oplus\ominus\hskip 4.2679pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}^{*}p_{2}^{*}}\right)^{\!*}
. Starting from the right side, we use the definition of
\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 8.53581pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
in Eq.(
67
)
and deduce the following:

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\oplus\ominus\hskip 5.69054pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}^{*}p_{2}^{*}}\right)^{\!*}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}z_{1}\text{d}z_{2}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{1}}e^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{2}}z_{1}^{p_{1}^{*}}z_{2}^{p_{2}^{*}}\hat{D}_{\oplus\ominus\,\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{k},z_{1},z_{2})\bigg]^{\!*}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}z_{1}\text{d}z_{2}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{1}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{2}}z_{1}^{p_{1}}z_{2}^{p_{2}}\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{D}_{\oplus\ominus\,\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{k},z_{1},z_{2})\Big]^{\!*}

(74)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}z_{1}\text{d}z_{2}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{1}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{2}}z_{1}^{p_{1}}z_{2}^{p_{2}}\!\left[\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt\check{D}_{\ominus\oplus\,{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k},z_{1},z_{2})\right]=-\hskip 0.85358pt\check{\mathcal{I}}_{\ominus\oplus\hskip 4.2679pt{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\,.

We can also verify the identity
(
73
)
for other choices of
(a,b)
similarly.

2.4.4
Rotation of Spinor Seed Integrals

In the above subsections, we have computed all these seed integrals by choosing the spatial momentum to be along the direction of
z
-axis (
\mathbf{k}\!=\!k\hat{z}\hskip 0.85358pt
). Since the seed integrals are spinor-tensors,
we can derive them for a general momentum direction by a proper
SU(2)
rotation.

Consider the spatial momentum in a general direction,

\hat{\,\mathbf{k}}=(\sin\theta\cos\varphi,\sin\theta\sin\varphi,\cos\theta)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(75)

The helicity spinor
h_{\alpha}(\hat{\,\mathbf{k}})
is in the
(\frac{1}{2},0)
representation of the Lorentz group. The corresponding rotation matrix for helicity spinor
h_{\alpha}(\mathbf{k})
from the
h_{\alpha}(\hat{z})
is given by

U(\theta,\varphi)=e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\varphi\sigma^{3}}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\theta\sigma^{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\varphi}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}&~-e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\varphi}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\\[4.2679pt]
e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\varphi}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}&~e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\varphi}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\end{pmatrix}\!,

(76)

which is a unitary matrix obeying
U^{\dagger}(\theta,\varphi)\!=\!U^{-1}(\theta,\varphi)\hskip 0.85358pt
.
Similarly, we can derive the rotation matrix for spinor
h^{\dagger}_{\dot{\alpha}}(\hat{\,\mathbf{k}})
, which belongs to the
(0,\frac{1}{2})
representation of the Lorentz group.
For a spinor tensor as direct product of two spinors (such as
M_{\alpha\beta}
), we apply
the rotation
U(\theta,\varphi)

to each of the spinor-tensor index. We summarize in Table
1

the rotation rules for six types of the spinor-tensors considered in the present analysis.

Tensor Type

General Rotation Rule

Matrix Form

M_{\alpha\beta}

M_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{k})\!=\!U_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}U_{\beta}{}^{\delta}M^{(z)}_{\gamma\delta}(k)

M(\mathbf{k})\!=\!UM^{(z)}U^{T}

M_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}

M_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}(\mathbf{k})\!=\!U^{*}_{\dot{\alpha}}{}^{\dot{\gamma}}U^{*}_{\dot{\beta}}{}^{\dot{\delta}}M^{(z)}_{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}(k)

M(\mathbf{k})\!=\!U^{*}M^{(z)}U^{\dagger}

M_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}

M_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}(\mathbf{k})\!=\!U_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}U^{*}_{\dot{\beta}}{}^{\dot{\delta}}M^{(z)}_{\gamma\dot{\delta}}(k)

M(\mathbf{k})\!=\!UM^{(z)}U^{\dagger}

M_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}

M_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}(\mathbf{k})\!=\!U_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}M^{(z)}_{\gamma}{}^{\delta}(k)(U^{-1})_{\delta}{}^{\beta}

M(\mathbf{k})\!=\!UM^{(z)}U^{-1}\!=\!UM^{(z)}U^{\dagger}

M^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}

M^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}(\mathbf{k})\!=\!\big((U^{\dagger})^{-1}\big)^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\gamma}}M^{(z)\dot{\gamma}}{}_{\dot{\delta}}(k)U^{*}_{\dot{\beta}}{}^{\dot{\delta}}

M(\mathbf{k})\!=\!(U^{\dagger})^{-1}M^{(z)}U^{\dagger}\!=\!UM^{(z)}U^{\dagger}

M^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}

M^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}(\mathbf{k})\!=\!\big((U^{\dagger})^{-1}\big)^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\gamma}}M^{(z)\dot{\gamma}\delta}(k)(U^{-1})_{\delta}{}^{\beta}

M(\mathbf{k})\!=\!(U^{\dagger})^{-1}M^{(z)}U^{-1}\!=\!UM^{(z)}U^{\dagger}

Table 1
:
Rotation rules for six types of the spinor tensors. The symbol
M^{(z)}
denotes the relevant result evaluated for the spatial momentum in the direction of
z
-axis.

Consider a special case
\varphi\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
,
where
\mathbf{k}
lies in the
x
-
z
plane and has an angle
\theta
relative to the
z
-axis,
namely,

\hat{\,\mathbf{k}}\!=\!(\sin\theta,0,\cos\theta)
. Thus, the rotation matrix reduces to

U(\theta,0)=e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\theta\sigma^{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}&-\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\\[4.2679pt]
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}&\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\end{pmatrix}\!.

(77)

Since
U(\theta,0)
is real, we have

U^{*}(\theta,0)\!=\!U(\theta,0)
and
U^{\dagger}(\theta,0)\!=\!U^{T}(\theta,0)\!=\!U^{-1}(\theta,0)\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the rotation for all types of spinor-tensors in Table
1
can be written in a single matrix form,

M(\mathbf{k})=U(\theta,0)\,M^{(z)}\,U(\theta,0)^{T}.

(78)

3
Cosmological Signatures of Right-Handed Neutrino during Inflation

After presenting the spinor coefficients, the Majorana fermion propagators, and the seed integrals within the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism in Section
2
, we then turn to the
loop-induced primordial signatures generated by the right-handed neutrino. The derivative interaction in Eq.(
14
) not only induces an effective chemical potential through the slow-roll inflaton background, but also couples the inflaton fluctuation
\delta\phi
directly to the right-handed neutrinos. In consequence, the heavy-neutrino loop leaves imprints on the inflaton bispectrum
\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime}
and can generate the non-analytic cosmological collider signals, as shown in Fig.
1
.

The rest of this section is organized as follows. In Section
3.1
, we present the inflaton three-point correlator generated by the right-handed-neutrino triangle loop and classify the eight distinct contractions. In Section
3.2
, we factorize the loop diagram into three sub-diagrams containing the left part, the right part, and the middle part (bubble) in the nonlocal sector, which will be computed separately. In Section
3.3
, we derive the final bispectrum, identify the dominant contributions, and analyze their angular dependence.

3.1
Three-Point Inflaton Correlator from Right-Handed Neutrino Loop

We discussed in Section
2.2
how the inflaton background
\phi_{0}
modifies the dynamics of the right-handed neutrino through the dimension-5 operator in Eq.(
14
). In addition, the inflaton fluctuation
\delta\phi
also couples to the right-handed neutrino through the same dimension-5 operator,

\Delta\mathcal{L}=\!\sqrt{-g\,}\frac{-1}{a(\tau)\Lambda}\partial_{\mu}\delta\phi\,N^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}N=\frac{\,-1\,}{\Lambda}\partial_{\mu}\delta\phi\,\tilde{N}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\tilde{N}\,,

(79)

where
\tilde{N}\!=\!a^{3/2}{N}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Through this interaction, the quantum fluctuation
\delta\phi
receives a backreaction from the right-handed neutrino
N
. This effect is ultimately encoded in the primordial non-Gaussianity through the three-point correlator of
\delta\phi
generated by the right-handed-neutrino triangle loop. The right-handed neutrino has a nonzero Majorana mass

m\!\gtrsim\!H
and a large chemical potential
\lambda\hskip-0.85358pt\thicksim\hskip-0.85358pt60H
. This loop contribution can generate a cosmological collider signal in the three-point correlator of
\delta\phi\hskip 0.85358pt
,

\displaystyle\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime}\,\thicksim\,A\!\left(\frac{k_{1}}{k_{3}}\right)^{\!\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}\!\!+\!\!\text{ H.c. }\!\hskip-0.85358pt,

(80)

where we have defined

\tilde{M}_{\!R}\!=\!{M_{\!R}}/{H}
,

\tilde{\lambda}\!=\!{\lambda}/{H}
,
and
\tilde{\nu}\!=\!\sqrt{\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{2}\!+\!\tilde{\lambda}^{2}\,}
.

In the SK formalism, the three-point correlator generated by the right-handed-neutrino loop
can be represented diagrammatically. Using the identity of the sigma matrices,

\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\dot{\alpha}\alpha}\!=\!\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\sigma^{\mu}_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
the inflaton-right-handed-neutrino interaction
term can be expressed as follows:

-\frac{1}{\Lambda}\partial_{\mu}\delta\phi\,\tilde{N}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\tilde{N}=\frac{1}{\Lambda}\partial_{\mu}\delta\phi\,\tilde{N}\sigma^{\mu}\tilde{N}^{\dagger}.

(81)

As a result, there are eight distinct contractions of the neutrino fields in the triangle loop. This means that for a fixed momentum configuration
(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2},\mathbf{k}_{3})
,
the three-point correlator receives contributions from eight diagrams, as shown in Fig.
2
.

(1)

(2)

(3)

(4)

(5)

(6)

(7)

(8)

Figure 2
:
Relevant diagrams arising from the right-handed neutrino loop. In this figure, each propagator of the right-handed neutrino,
such as
\langle\tilde{N}\tilde{N}^{\dagger}\rangle

and
\langle\tilde{N}\tilde{N}\rangle
,
is represented by a solid line with a single arrow or two reverse arrows. The line connecting a dot and a square without an arrow denotes a bulk-to-boundary propagator of the inflaton

\langle\delta\phi^{2}\rangle
. The square at one end marks the boundary point
(\tau_{\!f}\!\rightarrow\!0^{-})\,
. The shaded dot at the vertex indicates the two cases of time ordering in this vertex, including the time-ordered case “
\oplus
” and reverse-time-ordered case “
\ominus
”.

In the above diagrams, the external lines terminating at squares represent the inflaton propagator
\langle\delta\phi^{2}\rangle
, whereas the internal arrow-lines represent the neutrino propagators. The square at one end denotes the boundary point
(\tau_{\!f}\!\rightarrow\!0^{-})\,
. The shaded dot at each vertex indicates the two possible time-ordering prescriptions at that vertex: the time-ordered case “
\oplus
” and the reverse-time-ordered case “
\ominus
”. Using the SK path-integral formalism, the
three-point correlator of the inflaton,

\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime}
,
obtained by summing over the eight diagrams, can be derived as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{8}\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime(j)}

(82)

\displaystyle=\!\!\sum_{a,b,c=\pm}\!\!\!a\hskip 0.85358ptb\hskip 0.85358ptc\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!3}\!\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\!\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}\mathrm{d}\tau_{2}\mathrm{d}\tau_{3}\,\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\mathcal{F}_{\nu b}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})\mathcal{F}_{\lambda c}(\mathbf{k}_{3},\tau_{3})\!\!\int\!\!\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{q}}{\hskip 0.85358pt(2\pi)^{3}\,}\!\sum_{j=1}^{8}\!\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(j)\,,

where

\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime(j)}
denotes the contribution from the diagram
(j)
in the Fig.
2
,

a,b,c\!=\!\pm
are the SK labels, and the factor
(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt/\Lambda)^{3}
arises from the three interaction vertices. The vector function
\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k},\tau)
comes from the external leg of the inflaton field
\delta\phi(\mathbf{k})
and is given by

\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=\!\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}G_{a}(k;\tau)\\[2.84526pt]
\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{k}G_{a}(k;\tau)\end{pmatrix}=\frac{\,H^{2}\,}{\,2k^{3}\,}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}k^{2}\tau\\[2.84526pt]
\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{k}(1-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptak\tau)\end{pmatrix}\!e^{+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptak\tau},

(83)

where
G_{a}(k;\tau)
denotes the boundary-to-bulk propagator of the inflaton field treated
as an approximately massless scalar field, shown in the Eq.(
252
) in the Appendix
D

G_{a}(k;\tau)=\frac{\,H^{2}\,}{2k^{3}}(1-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pta\hskip 0.85358ptk\tau)e^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pta\hskip 0.85358ptk\tau}\,.

(84)

In Eq.(
82
),
the functions
\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(j)
(with
j=1,2,\cdots,8)
represent the neutrino triangle loop associated with the
i
-th diagram in Fig.
2
and
take the following forms,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(1)=

\displaystyle-\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}D_{ab\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\bar{\sigma}^{\nu{\dot{\beta}}\beta}D_{bc\beta{\dot{\gamma}}}(\mathbf{p}_{23},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}D_{ca\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{31},\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(2)=

\displaystyle-\bar{\sigma}^{\nu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}D_{ba\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{21},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\beta}}\beta}D_{ac\beta{\dot{\gamma}}}(\mathbf{p}_{13},\tau_{1},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}D_{cb\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{32},\tau_{3},\tau_{2})\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(3)=

\displaystyle\,\,\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}D_{ab\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\bar{\sigma}^{\nu{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\hat{D}_{bc\beta}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\gamma}(\mathbf{p}_{23},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\sigma^{\lambda}_{\,\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}\check{D}_{ca\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}(\mathbf{p}_{31},\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(4)=

\displaystyle\,\,\bar{\sigma}^{\nu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}D_{ba\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{21},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\hat{D}_{ac\beta}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\gamma}(\mathbf{p}_{13},\tau_{1},\tau_{3})\sigma^{\lambda}_{\,\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}\check{D}_{cb\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}^{\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}(\mathbf{p}_{32},\tau_{3},\tau_{2})\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(5)=

\displaystyle\,\,\sigma^{\nu}_{\,\,\,\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\check{D}_{ba\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}^{\,\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{21},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\beta}}\beta}D_{ac\beta{\dot{\gamma}}}(\mathbf{p}_{13},\tau_{1},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}\hat{D}_{cb\gamma}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\alpha}(\mathbf{p}_{32},\tau_{3},\tau_{2})\,,

(85)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(6)=

\displaystyle\,\,\sigma^{\mu}_{\,\,\,\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\check{D}_{ab\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\bar{\sigma}^{\nu{\dot{\beta}}\beta}D_{bc\beta{\dot{\gamma}}}(\mathbf{p}_{23},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}\hat{D}_{ca\gamma}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\alpha}(\mathbf{p}_{31},\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(7)=

\displaystyle\,\,\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hat{D}_{ab\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\sigma^{\nu}_{\,\,\,\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\check{D}_{bc\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{23},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}D_{ca\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{31},\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{Y}_{abc}^{\mu\nu\lambda}(8)=

\displaystyle\,\,\bar{\sigma}^{\nu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hat{D}_{ba\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{p}_{21},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})\sigma^{\mu}_{\,\,\,\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\check{D}_{ac\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{13},\tau_{1},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}D_{cb\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{32},\tau_{3},\tau_{2})\,,

where
\mathbf{p}_{12}\!=\!-\mathbf{p}_{21}\!=\!\mathbf{q}\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{1}\hskip 0.85358pt
,

\mathbf{p}_{23}\!=\!-\mathbf{p}_{32}\!=\!\mathbf{q}\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
\mathbf{p}_{31}\!=\!-\mathbf{p}_{13}\!=\!\mathbf{q}
, and
\mathbf{q}
is the loop momentum. The propagators with upper indices including
\hat{D}_{ab\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}
and
\check{D}_{ab\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{ab\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle\equiv\epsilon^{\beta\gamma}\hat{D}_{ab\alpha\gamma}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=-\hat{D}_{ab\alpha\gamma}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\epsilon^{\gamma\beta}\,,

(86)

\displaystyle\check{D}_{ab\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle\equiv\epsilon^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\gamma}}}\check{D}_{ab{\dot{\gamma}}{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=-\epsilon^{{\dot{\gamma}}{\dot{\alpha}}}\check{D}_{ab{\dot{\gamma}}{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\,.

We note that in the literature
23
;
37
;
8
only the first two of the eight diagrams
in Fig.
2

were considered. In these works
23
;
37
;
8
, the hard internal line
|\mathbf{p}_{12}|
was estimated using the saddle-point approximation, and the loop momentum was effectively fixed around a characteristic value. The saddle-point approximation may capture the leading exponential dependence,
but could miss important fluctuation prefactors around the saddle [as will be shown in Fig.
4
].
In this work, we include all eight diagrams shown in
Fig.
2
and perform a complete analysis of these diagrams.

3.2
Calculation of Three-Point Inflaton Correlator

In this subsection, we calculate the three-point correlator associated with the triangle loop shown in Fig.
2
. We will extract the cosmological collider signal from the three-point correlator in the squeezed limit
k_{3}\!\ll\!k_{1},k_{2}\,
.

We consider a specific momentum configuration in which
\mathbf{k}_{1}
,
\mathbf{k}_{2}
, and
\mathbf{k}_{3}
are nearly collinear along the
z
-axis:

\mathbf{k}_{1}\!\simeq\!-\mathbf{k}_{2}\,,\qquad\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!(0,0,k_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\mathbf{k}_{3}\!=\!(0,0,k_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(87)

Then, we can consider the case with the angle
\theta
between
\mathbf{k}_{1}
and
\mathbf{k}_{3}
being arbitrary and the
\mathbf{k}_{3}
being along the
z
-axis. In this case, we have

\mathbf{k}_{1}\!\simeq\!-\mathbf{k}_{2}\,,\qquad\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!k_{1}(\sin\theta,0,\cos\theta)\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\mathbf{k}_{3}\!=\!(0,0,k_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(88)

As discussed in Section
D.2
, the cosmological collider signal contains both local and nonlocal contributions, where
these two contributions have different physical origins and can be distinguished more clearly in the four-point correlator. Although their physical origins are different, both the local and nonlocal signals exhibit the same scaling behavior
\left(\!\frac{k_{1}}{k_{3}}\!\right)^{\!\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}}

in the three-point correlator. For the present analysis,
we will focus on the nonlocal contribution and extract the nonlocal part
of the fermion propagator.

In this subsection, we take the diagram-(7) in Fig.
2

as a sample for illustration and present a complete calculation for it. The same procedure can be applied to the other seven diagrams
in Fig.
2
. For simplicity, we set the Hubble parameter during inflation,
H
, to unity (
H\!=\!1
) in this subsection.

3.2.1
Factorization of Fermion Loop Diagrams

To extract nonlocal cosmological collider signals from the fermion loop, it is useful to isolate the kinematic region in which two internal lines simultaneously become soft. In analogy with cosmological collider signals from scalar loop, one expects that the non-analytic part of the diagram should arise precisely from this soft region and admit a factorized description in terms of left, right, and bubble subdiagrams.

For scalar loops, this factorization can be implemented directly. It has been shown that the non-analytic behavior of a loop diagram originates from the kinematic region in which two specific internal momenta simultaneously become soft
57
. In this limit, the corresponding internal lines can be effectively cut, and the diagram factorizes into three parts: a left tree-level subdiagram
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}
, a right tree-level subdiagram
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}
, and a bubble loop subdiagram
\mathcal{B}
in the middle. Accordingly, the nonlocal cosmological collider signal generated by a scalar loop takes the following schematic form
57
:

\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt0}\langle\delta\phi^{n}(\mathbf{k}_{i})\rangle^{\prime}_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}\sim\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}\!\big(\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathbf{k}^{(\mathsf{L})}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)\!\times\!\mathcal{B}(k_{s})\!\times\!\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}\!\big(\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathbf{k}^{(\mathsf{R})}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(89)

Following the same logic, for the fermionic triangle diagrams in Fig.
2
, the internal momenta
\mathbf{p}_{31}
and
\mathbf{p}_{23}
can be taken to the soft limit,
\mathbf{p}_{31}\,,\mathbf{p}_{23}\!\rightarrow\!{O}(k_{s})
, which corresponds to the late-time limit described in Eq.(
37
). For the nonlocal contribution, we expect the three-point correlator in the squeezed-limit to have the following factorized form:
4
4

4

The factorization can be derived by taking the folded limit as shown in Appendix
C.3
.

\displaystyle\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt0}\langle\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{1})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{2})\delta\phi(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle^{\prime}_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}\simeq\sum_{d=\pm}\!\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}_{(d)}\!(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{B}_{(d)}(\mathbf{k}_{s})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}_{(d)}\!(\mathbf{k}_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(90)

where
\mathbf{k}_{s}\!=\!\mathbf{k}_{1}\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{2}\!=\!-\mathbf{k}_{3}

for the three-point correlator.

On the other hand, for a fermion loop, the factorization is more subtle than in the scalar case. The reason is that the nonlocal parts of the propagators
D_{\ominus\oplus}
and
D_{\oplus\ominus}
are no longer identical, owing to the helicity-dependent spinor structure carried by the fermion propagators. As a result, the theta-function dependence in the time-ordered propagators
D_{\oplus\oplus}
and
D_{\ominus\ominus}
cannot be eliminated in the same way as for scalar loops, hence a direct application of the scalar cutting rule is not available.

To isolate a factorized nonlocal sector, we decompose the non-time-ordered propagators into their symmetric (S) and antisymmetric (A) parts:

\displaystyle D^{\mathrm{(S)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{~D_{\ominus\oplus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\!+\!D_{\oplus\ominus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})~}{2}\,,

(91)

\displaystyle D^{\mathrm{(A)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{~D_{\ominus\oplus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\!-\!D_{\oplus\ominus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})~}{2}\,.

Then, the time-ordered propagators can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle D_{\oplus\oplus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=D^{\mathrm{(S)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\!+\!D^{\mathrm{(A)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\,(\theta_{12}\!-\!\theta_{21})\,,

(92)

\displaystyle D_{\ominus\ominus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=D^{\mathrm{(S)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\!+\!D^{\mathrm{(A)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\,(\theta_{21}\!-\!\theta_{12})\,,

where
\theta_{ij}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\theta(\tau_{i}\!-\!\tau_{j})\,.

The symmetric part does not contain theta functions and thus can be factorized directly,
whereas the antisymmetric part still depends on theta functions and cannot be easily factorized. In this analysis, we extract the loop contribution in which the soft internal propagator
is taken to be its symmetric (non-time-ordered) part. A systematic treatment of the antisymmetric part is technically challenging,
so we will use the symmetric part for estimate and tackle the problem of
the antisymmetric part in future work. This differs from the scalar case as discussed in Appendix
D.1
. For the scalar propagators, the nonlocal parts of
D_{\ominus\oplus}
and
D_{\oplus\ominus}
are equal,

\displaystyle\left[D_{\oplus\ominus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}=\left[D_{\ominus\oplus}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}=\left[\sigma_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})\sigma^{*}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}

(93)

\displaystyle=\frac{H^{2}(\tau_{1}\tau_{2})^{3/2}}{4\pi}\left[\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu})\big]^{2}\!\left(\frac{k^{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}}{4}\right)^{\!\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}\!\!+\!\big[\Gamma(\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu})\big]^{2}\!\left(\frac{k^{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}}{4}\right)^{\!\!-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}\right]\!.

Hence the nonlocal part of the antisymmetric combination vanishes automatically for scalar propagators. But for the fermion case, the presence of two sets of spinor coefficients
together with the spinor-helicity structure makes

\left[D^{\mathrm{(A)}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}

nonzero. This directly obstructs the factorization.

In Section
2.3
, we introduced three types of propagators for the two-component fermion field. Their symmetric nonlocal parts in the late-time limit take
the following form:

\displaystyle\left[D^{\mathrm{(S)}}_{\hskip 8.04996pt\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{~\sum_{s}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[u_{s}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{k})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{k})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}~}{2}\,\sigma_{\!0,\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{\,\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{2}\sinh(\pi\tilde{\lambda})\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\tilde{\nu})\big]^{2}\,}{\,\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu})\,\Gamma\!\left(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}\right)\,}\left(4k^{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\right)^{\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!(\tilde{\nu}\!\rightarrow\!-\tilde{\nu})\right]\!\sigma_{\!0,\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}\,,

(94a)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{D}^{\mathrm{(S)}\,\,\,\beta}_{\hskip 8.04996pt\alpha}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{~\sum_{s}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[u_{s}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{k})v_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{k})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}~}{2}\,\delta_{\alpha}^{\,\,\,\beta}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{\,\tilde{M}_{\!R}\,\cosh(\pi\tilde{\lambda})\,\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\tilde{\nu})\big]^{2}\,}{\,\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu})\,\Gamma\!\left(-\mathrm{i}\tilde{\lambda}-\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}\right)\,}\left(4k^{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\right)^{\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!(\tilde{\nu}\!\rightarrow\!-\tilde{\nu})\right]\!\delta_{\alpha}^{\,\,\,\beta}\,,

(94b)

\displaystyle\left[\check{D}^{\mathrm{(S)}{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\hskip 16.09991pt\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{~\sum_{s}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[v_{s}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{k})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{k})\right]_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}~}{2}\,\delta^{{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{~\tilde{M}_{\!R}\,\cosh(\pi\tilde{\lambda})\,\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\tilde{\nu})\big]^{2}~}{\,\Gamma(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu})\,\Gamma\!\left(\mathrm{i}\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}\right)\,}\left(4k^{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\right)^{\mathrm{i}\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!(\tilde{\nu}\!\rightarrow\!-\tilde{\nu})\right]\!\delta^{{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}\,.

(94c)

In the above, one spinor index in the second and third propagators
on the left side of each equality sign
has been raised using Eq.(
86
),
and the explicit expressions for

\left[u_{s}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{k})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{k})\right]_{\!\mathrm{(NLoc)}}\!

are given in Eq.(
40
). An important simplification is that the symmetric nonlocal parts
of all three types of propagators are proportional to the
identity matrix in spinor space, namely,
\sigma_{\!0,\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}
,

\delta_{\alpha}^{\,\,\,\beta}
, or
\delta^{{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}
,
and thus they do not depend on the direction of the helicity spinor.

For the symmetric part, the nested time-integrals over

\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
become factorized,

\iint\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}\!\longrightarrow\!\!\int\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\!\int\!\text{d}\tau_{2}\,,

because the soft propagators are reduced to products of functions of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
without theta-function dependence. In this sense, the two soft internal lines can be effectively cut even for the fermion loop. The cutting rule and its applicability in the present context are explained
in Appendix
C.3
. We note that for the non-time-ordered soft internal propagator
D_{\ominus\oplus}
or
D_{\oplus\ominus}
,
it can be factorized directly and thus we keep their original form without decomposition into the symmetric and antisymmetric parts.

Figure 3
:
After applying the factorization method, the diagram-(7) in Fig.
2

can be decomposed into a left subdiagram, a bubble loop, and a right subdiagram.

With the above prescription, the factorizan of the fermion loop can be implemented diagram by diagram. As an example, we illustrate in Fig.
3
the decomposition of diagram-(7) in Fig.
2
. In this decomposition, all time integrals are assigned to the left and right parts. The left subdiagram
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}_{(d)}
is a two-point object containing the time integral

\iint\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}
together with the time-dependent factors
(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}
and
(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}

inherited from the two soft lines. The right subdiagram
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}_{(d)}
contains only the time integral

\int\!\text{d}\tau_{3}
and the corresponding factor
(-\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}
. In contrast, the bubble factor
\mathcal{B}_{(d)}
contains only the loop integral over

\text{d}\mathbf{q}
, together with the residual Gamma-function coefficients in the two soft propagators. All non-analytic dependence arises from evaluating this bubble factor in the soft limit

\mathbf{p}_{31},\mathbf{p}_{23}\!\rightarrow\!{O}(k_{s})\hskip 0.85358pt
.

We specify the SK ordering of the three vertices explicitly and
denote the three parts by

\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}_{(d),ab}
,
\mathcal{B}_{(d),abc}
,
and
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}_{(d),c}
, respectively. Thus, we express the nonlocal contribution to the squeezed-limit three-point correlator
as follows:

\displaystyle\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow 0}\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime}_{\mathrm{(NLoc)}}\simeq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c=\pm\\
d=\pm\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!a\hskip 0.85358ptb\hskip 0.85358ptc\,\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}_{(d),ab}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{B}_{(d),abc}(\mathbf{k}_{s})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}_{(d),c}(\mathbf{k}_{3})\,.

(95)

For the diagram-(7), the left subdiagram is a two-point correlator containing two external inflaton legs,

\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})
and
\mathcal{F}_{\nu b}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})
,
together with one hard internal fermion propagator
\hat{D}_{ab}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
,
which takes the following form:

\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}_{(d),ab}=\!\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\!\!\int\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}\,\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\hat{D}_{ab}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma^{\nu}\mathcal{F}_{\nu b}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,,

(96)

where the factors
(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}
arise from the late-time behavior of the two soft lines in Eq.(
51
), and the momenta satisfy
\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!-\mathbf{k}_{2}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\mathbf{p}_{12}\hskip 0.85358pt
.
The right subdiagram contains no internal fermion propagator and it can expressed as follows:

\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{R})}_{(d),c}=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)\!\int\!\text{d}\tau_{3}\,\mathcal{F}_{\lambda c}(\mathbf{k}_{3},\tau_{3})\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda}(-\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}\,,

(97)

where the factor
(-\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}
also comes from the two soft lines.

We note that the bubble factor depends only on the loop momentum and
the residual Gamma-function coefficients from the two soft propagators. Thus, we can express it in the following form:

\mathcal{B}_{(d),abc}=\!\int\!\!\frac{\text{d}^{3}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{3}}(2q)^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}\Big|2(\mathbf{q}\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{s})\Big|^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}\{\Gamma\}_{(d),abc}\,,

(98)

where
\{\Gamma\}_{(d),abc}
denotes the remaining Gamma-function factors associated with the two soft lines.

In the following subsections, we explicitly compute the diagram-(7) as a representative example. The analysis of the remaining diagrams of Fig.
2
can be carried in the same fashion.

3.2.2
Left Part of the Diagram

We begin with the left subdiagram of diagram-(7) as shown in Fig.
3
. This two-point subdiagram contributes to
\mathcal{T}_{(d),ab}^{(\mathsf{L})}

and consists of two massless external inflaton lines,
\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})

and
\mathcal{F}_{\nu b}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})
, together with one massive internal fermion propagator

\hat{D}_{ab}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
. It carries two spinor indices and takes the following form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),ab\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}\,(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}

(99)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{F}_{\mu a}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\,\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,\hat{D}_{ab\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\,\sigma^{\nu}_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\,\mathcal{F}_{\nu b}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})\,,

where the factor
(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}
with
d=\pm
comes from the time dependence
of the soft lines in the bubble subdiagram
\mathcal{B}_{(d)}
, and the momentum satisfies

\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!-\mathbf{k}_{2}\!=\!\mathbf{p}_{12}
. For simplicity, we choose
\mathbf{k}_{1}
to be along the direction of
z
-axis,

\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!(0,0,k_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt
. The result for a general orientation of
\mathbf{k}_{1}
can be obtained
by the rotation rule derived in Section
2.4.4
.

In Refs.
23
;
37
, the hard internal propagator
\hat{D}_{ab}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
was evaluated
using a saddle-point approximation around,

|2k_{1}\tau_{1}|\!=\!|2k_{1}\tau_{2}|\simeq\tilde{M}_{\!R}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
in the regime
\tilde{\lambda}\gg\tilde{M}_{\!R}
. In contrast, we introduced in Section
2.4

a set of seed integrals that allows one to evaluate the left subdiagram directly. To express Eq.(
99
) in terms of the seed integrals
\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 5.69054pt\alpha\beta}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
,
we substitute the external lines defined in Eq.(
83
)
into Eq.(
99
), and contract the Lorentz indices between

\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
and
\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}
(or
\sigma^{\nu}
). For instance, we can derive:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mu\oplus}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{0\oplus}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{0}\!+\!\mathcal{F}_{3\oplus}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{3}

(100)

\displaystyle=\frac{\,\tau_{1}e^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptk_{1}\tau_{1}}\,}{2k_{1}}\bar{\sigma}^{0}\!+\!\frac{\,\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pte^{\mathrm{i}k_{1}\tau_{1}}(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptk_{1}\tau_{1})\,}{2k_{1}^{2}}\bar{\sigma}^{3}\,.

In this way,
\mathcal{F}_{\mu\oplus}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}
is rewritten as
a sum of terms with definite power-law dependence on
\tau_{1}
. Matching the resulting expression
to the definition of the seed integrals in Eq.(
67
), we decompose the left subdiagram
into a linear combination of seed integrals with different powers of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

As an illustration, we consider the case
a=b=\oplus
and derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),\oplus\oplus\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\mathcal{F}_{\mu\oplus}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hat{D}_{\oplus\oplus\alpha}^{\hskip 8.04996pt\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\sigma^{\nu}_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\mathcal{F}_{\nu\oplus}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\!\frac{1}{\,4(k_{1}k_{2})^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,}\Big[(\bar{\sigma}^{0}\!+\!\bar{\sigma}^{3})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\oplus\oplus}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}(\sigma^{0}\!-\!\sigma^{3})_{\!\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\sigma}^{3{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\oplus\oplus}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}(\sigma^{0}\!-\!\sigma^{3})_{\!\beta{\dot{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{\sigma}^{0}+\bar{\sigma}^{3})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\oplus\oplus}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}\sigma^{3}_{\,\,\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\!+\!\bar{\sigma}^{3{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\oplus\oplus}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}\,\sigma^{3}_{\,\,\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\!\frac{1}{\,4(k_{1}k_{2})^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,}\!\!\Bigg[\!-\!S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,\,1}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,1}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\!\left(-S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!4S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\right)\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,2}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,2}\Bigg]\,,

(101)

where

\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 8.19447pt\alpha}^{p_{1},p_{2}\,\,\,\beta}=-\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{ab\hskip 8.19447pt\alpha\gamma}^{p_{1},p_{2}}\,\epsilon^{\gamma\beta}\hskip 0.85358pt
. We also use the following matrix notation,

\displaystyle\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,1}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,1}=\bigg(\begin{matrix}\,1\,&\,0\,\,\\
\,0\,&\,0\,\,\end{matrix}\bigg)^{\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}},\qquad\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,2}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,2}=\bigg(\begin{matrix}\,0\,&\,0\,\,\\
\,0\,&\,1\,\,\end{matrix}\bigg)^{\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}\,.

(102)

Substituting the seed integrals
S_{1}^{p_{1},p_{2}}
and
S_{2}^{p_{1},p_{2}}

(with
p_{1},p_{2}\!=\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\,1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}
)
into Eq.(
3.2.2
), we obtain the explicit result for the left subdiagram

\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),\oplus\oplus\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}
.

The case
a\!=\!b\!=\!\ominus
can be treated in the same manner. The corresponding subdiagram,
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),\ominus\ominus\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}
,
can be expressed in terms of the seed integrals
S_{3}^{p_{1},p_{2}}
and
S_{4}^{p_{1},p_{2}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),\ominus\ominus\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,\mathcal{F}_{\mu\ominus}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hat{D}_{\ominus\ominus\alpha}^{\hskip 8.04996pt\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{p}_{12},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\sigma^{\nu}_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\mathcal{F}_{\nu\ominus}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\frac{1}{\,4(k_{1}k_{2})^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,}\Big[(\bar{\sigma}^{0}\!-\!\bar{\sigma}^{3})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\ominus\ominus}^{1-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}(\sigma^{0}\!+\!\sigma^{3})_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\sigma}^{3{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\ominus\ominus}^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}(\sigma^{0}\!+\!\sigma^{3})_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{\sigma}^{0}\!-\!\bar{\sigma}^{3})\,(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\ominus\ominus}^{1-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}\,\sigma^{3}_{\,\,\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\!+\!\bar{\sigma}^{3{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{\ominus\ominus}^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}})^{\,\,\,\,\beta}_{\alpha}\sigma^{3}_{\,\,\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=3}^{4}\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\frac{1}{\,4(k_{1}k_{2})^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,}\Bigg[\!-\!S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,1}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,1}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\!\left(-S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!4S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\right)\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,2}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,2}\Bigg]\,.

(103)

We next consider diagram-(8) in Fig.
2
, which is related to diagram-(7) by reversing the fermion flow. Thus the same factorization can be applied. Its left subdiagram is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8){\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),ab\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\text{d}\tau_{1}\text{d}\tau_{2}\,(-\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}(-\tau_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}

(104)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{F}_{\mu b}(\mathbf{k}_{2},\tau_{2})\bar{\sigma}^{\mu{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hat{D}_{ba\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(\mathbf{p}_{21},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})\sigma^{\nu}_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\mathcal{F}_{\nu a}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{1})\,,

where
\mathbf{p}_{21}\!=\!-\mathbf{p}_{12}\!=\!-\mathbf{k}_{1}
. Because the fermion flow of diagram-(8) is reversed relative to diagram-(7),
its internal momentum is given by
\mathbf{p}_{21}\!=\!-\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!(0,0,-k_{1})
,
and its time-integral variables are interchanged (
\tau_{1}\!\leftrightarrow\!\tau_{2}
). In Eq.(
104
), the propagator

\hat{D}_{ba\alpha}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\beta}(-\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})

can be obtained from the corresponding propagator

\hat{D}_{ba\,\gamma}^{\hskip 14.22636pt\delta}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})

through a rotation with
\theta\!=\!\varphi\!=\!\pi
as follows:

\hat{D}_{ba\,\alpha}^{\hskip 14.22636pt\beta}(-\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})=U(\pi,\pi)_{\alpha}^{\,\,\,\gamma}\hat{D}_{ba\,\gamma}^{\hskip 14.22636pt\delta}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{1})U(\pi,\pi)_{\,\,\delta}^{\dagger\,\,\,\beta}\,.

(105)

Accordingly, the nested-integral cases
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8)}_{(d),\oplus\oplus}
and

\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8)}_{(d),\ominus\ominus}
are obtained from the results of diagram-(7)
by exchanging
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
and performing a rotation
U(\pi,\pi)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8)\hskip 5.69054pt{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),\oplus\oplus\hskip 5.69054pt{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})

\displaystyle=U(\pi,\pi)^{{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\,\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!{\dot{\gamma}}}_{(d),\oplus\oplus\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\dot{\delta}}\bigg|_{\tau_{1}\leftrightarrow\tau_{2}}\!\!(\mathbf{k}_{1})\,U(\pi,\pi)^{\dagger\,\dot{\delta}}_{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\frac{1}{\,4(k_{1}k_{2})^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,}\!\Bigg[\!-\!S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,2}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\!\left(\!-S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!4S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\right)\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,1}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,1}\Bigg]\!,

(106a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8)\hskip 5.69054pt{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),\ominus\ominus\hskip 5.69054pt{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})

\displaystyle=U(\pi,\pi)^{{\dot{\alpha}}}_{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\,\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!{\dot{\gamma}}}_{(d),\ominus\ominus\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\dot{\delta}}\bigg|_{\tau_{1}\leftrightarrow\tau_{2}}\!\!(\mathbf{k}_{1})\,U(\pi,\pi)^{\dagger\,\dot{\delta}}_{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=3}^{4}\!\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!\frac{1}{\,4(k_{1}k_{2})^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\,}\!\Bigg[\!-\!S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,1}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,1}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\left(\!-S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\!+\!4S_{\ell}^{1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu},1+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}\right)\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\,\,\,2}\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\,\,\,2}\Bigg]\!.

(106b)

Here the notation
\left[\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)}\big|_{\tau_{1}\!\leftrightarrow\tau_{2}}\right]

means that the roles of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
are exchanged in the corresponding time integrals.

An important observation is that the dominant contribution to
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L})}_{(d),ab}

corresponds to the cases with
a\hskip 0.85358ptd,b\hskip 0.85358ptd\!>\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. This can be understood by inspecting
the following integral (which is contained in the time integral of the left subdiagram):

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}z\,e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptaz}z^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}=-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pta\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}z\,e^{-z}(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptaz)^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}=-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pta\,e^{\frac{\pi(ad)\tilde{\nu}}{2}}\,\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu})\,.

(107)

From the above, we see that when
a\hskip 0.85358ptd,b\hskip 0.85358ptd\!<\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
,
the integral is exponentially suppressed. In consequence, the left subdiagram is dominated by contributions with

a\hskip 0.85358ptd,b\hskip 0.85358ptd\!>\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Finally, when the momentum of the propagator in the left subdiagram
is not aligned with the
z
-axis, we define
\theta
as the polar angle between
\mathbf{p}_{12}\!=\!\mathbf{k}_{1}
and the
z
-axis, namely

\mathbf{k}_{1}\!=\!k_{1}(\sin\theta,0,\cos\theta)
and

\mathbf{k}_{1}\!\simeq\!-\mathbf{k}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the corresponding left subdiagram for nonzero
\theta

can be obtained from the
\theta\!=\!0
case by the rotation rule
given in Section
2.4.4
.

3.2.3
Right Part of the Diagram

The right subdiagram is considerably simpler than the left one. It can be viewed as a degenerate “one-point” function, which is
a
2\!\times\!2
spinor matrix with no internal fermion propagator. As an explicit example, we consider the right subdiagram of diagram-(7)
and it is given by the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(d),c}^{(\mathsf{R},7){\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}=\left(\!\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\right)\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\text{d}\tau_{3}\,\mathcal{F}_{\lambda c}(\mathbf{k}_{3},\tau_{3})\,\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}(-\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}\,,

(108)

where the factor
(-\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}
with
d\!=\!\pm
comes from
the time dependence of the two soft lines. The corresponding expressions for the other diagrams can be derived in the same way.

Since the right subdiagram contains only one massless external line

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda c}(\mathbf{k}_{3},\tau_{3})
,
the time integral can be directly evaluated by using the integral formula,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\text{d}t\,t^{z}e^{-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\omega\hskip 0.85358ptt}=(\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\omega)^{-1-z}\hskip 0.85358pt\Gamma(1\!+\!z)\,.

(109)

Thus, we derive the contribution to the right subdiagram as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(d),\oplus}^{(\mathsf{R},7){\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{\,2\Lambda\,}\!\right)\!k_{3}^{-3-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}e^{d\pi\tilde{\nu}}\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}\end{pmatrix}^{\!\!{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma},

(110)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(d),\ominus}^{(\mathsf{R},7){\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}

\displaystyle=\left(\!\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{\,2\Lambda\,}\!\right)\!k_{3}^{-3-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}e^{-d\pi\tilde{\nu}}\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\begin{pmatrix}3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}^{\!\!{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}.

Eq.(
110
) shows that the right subdiagram could avoid the additional
exponential suppression factor
e^{-2\pi\tilde{\nu}}
only when

d\!=\!c\!=\!\pm
. Accordingly, in the following sections
we will only consider the cases with
d\!=\!c\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Among the eight diagrams in Fig.
2
, there are six diagrams
(with numbers 1,2,5,6,7,8) in which their right subdiagram takes the same form. The only exceptions are diagrams (3) and (4) which take a different form and
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(d),c\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}^{(\mathsf{R},3)}=\mathcal{T}_{(d),c\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}^{(\mathsf{R},4)}=\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0^{-}}\!\!\!\text{d}\tau_{3}\,\mathcal{F}_{\lambda c}(\mathbf{k}_{3},\tau_{3})\,\sigma^{\lambda}_{\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}(-\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358ptd\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}\,.

(111)

These can be obtained from the counterparts of the diagram-(7) by exchanging
the diagonal elements of the
2\!\times\!2
spinor matrix. Thus, using the formula for diagram-(7) we derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(d),\oplus\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}^{(\mathsf{R},3)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{(d),\oplus\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}^{(\mathsf{R},4)}=\left(\!\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{\,2\Lambda\,}\!\right)\!k_{3}^{-3-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}e^{d\pi\tilde{\nu}}\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\!\!\begin{pmatrix}3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}_{\!\!\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}},

(112a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(d),\ominus\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}^{(\mathsf{R},3)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{(d),\ominus\,\,\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}^{(\mathsf{R},4)}=\left(\!\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{\,2\Lambda\,}\!\right)\!k_{3}^{-3-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}e^{-d\pi\tilde{\nu}}\Gamma(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\!\!\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}\end{pmatrix}_{\!\!\gamma{\dot{\gamma}}}.

(112b)

3.2.4
Central Part: Fermion Bubble Contribution

In this subsection, we analyze the bubble contribution
\mathcal{B}_{(d),abc}
,
which contains the loop-momentum integral
\displaystyle\int\!\text{d}^{3}\mathbf{q}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
the dependence on the soft external momentum
\mathbf{k}_{s}
, and the remaining Gamma-function factors from the nonlocal parts of the two soft internal lines.

The treatment of the two internal lines in the bubble depends on the SK structure. In the time-ordered sector, for example when
a\!=\!b\!=\!c
, we use the symmetric component for the two internal lines, rather than the nonlocal propagators themselves. In a non-time-ordered sector, on the other hand, such as
a\!=\!b\!=\!\ominus
and
c\!=\!\oplus
, the bubble diagram is constructed from the corresponding non-time-ordered propagator,
D_{\ominus\oplus}
or
D_{\oplus\ominus}
, and we retain its nonlocal part. Moreover, as shown in the previous two subsections, the left subdiagram is dominated by the sector
d=a=b
, while the right subdiagram is dominated by
d=c
. It then follows that the leading contribution to the full diagram comes from the sector
d=a=b=c
, whereas the remaining choices of
a
,
b
, and
c
are suppressed.

In the following, we use the two representative cases

\begin{cases}d=-,\quad a=b=c=\ominus\,,\\
d=+,\quad a=b=c=\oplus\,,\end{cases}

(113)

to illustrate the evaluation of the bubble contribution.

In the diagram-(7), the central part of the bubble loop integral
contains the product of two propagators with the spinor structure:

\check{D}_{ac\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}D_{cb\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.04996pt\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),abc\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Since
a\!=\!b\!=\!c\hskip 0.85358pt
, the two soft internal propagators entering
the bubble loop integration should be taken to be the nonlocal parts
of the symmetric propagators, as discussed in
Section
3.2.1
. Thus, we have the following:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\left[\check{D}_{bc\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}^{\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}(\mathbf{p}_{23},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)}}

\displaystyle\!\Rightarrow\!\left[\check{D}^{\mathrm{(S)}{\dot{\beta}}}_{\mathrm{\hskip 15.39981pt\!\!\!{\dot{\gamma}}}}(\mathbf{p}_{23};\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)}}\!=\dfrac{\,\sum_{s}\!\big[v_{s}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{p}_{23})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{23})\big]_{\!\mathrm{(NLoc)}}\,}{2}\,\delta^{{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\,,

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Big[D_{ca\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{31},\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\Big]_{\text{(NLoc)}}

\displaystyle\!\Rightarrow\!\left[D^{\mathrm{(S)}}_{\hskip 8.04996pt\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{31};\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)}}\!=\dfrac{\,\sum_{s}\!\big[u_{s}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{31})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{p}_{31})\big]_{\!\mathrm{(NLoc)}}\,}{2}\,\sigma_{0\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\,.

(114)

For fixed
d
, the bubble loop contribution
\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),abc\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}

(with
d\!=\!a\!=\!b\!=\!c
) can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.04996pt\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),abc\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}=\!\!\int\!\!\frac{\,\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{q}\,}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\,\left[\check{D}^{\mathrm{(S)}{\dot{\beta}}}_{\mathrm{\hskip 7.6999pt~~{\dot{\gamma}}}}(\mathbf{p}_{23};\tau_{2},\tau_{3})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)},d}\,}{(\tau_{2}\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}}\frac{\,\left[D^{\mathrm{(S)}}_{\hskip 8.04996pt\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}(\mathbf{p}_{31};\tau_{3},\tau_{1})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)},d}\,}{(\tau_{3}\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\sigma_{0\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\!\int\!\!\frac{\,\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{q}\,}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\,\sum_{s}\!\!\left[v_{s}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{p}_{23})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{23})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)},d}\,}{2(\tau_{2}\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}}\frac{\,\sum_{s}\!\!\left[u_{s}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{31})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{p}_{31})\right]_{\text{(NLoc)},d}\,}{2(\tau_{3}\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}}\,,

(115)

where

\mathbf{p}_{23}=\mathbf{q}\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{s}\,,\mathbf{p}_{31}=\mathbf{q}\,.

The nonlocal mode-function combinations

\Big[v_{s}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{p}_{23})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{23})\Big]_{\!\mathrm{(NLoc)},d}

and

\Big[u_{s}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{31})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{p}_{31})\Big]_{\!\mathrm{(NLoc)},d}\hskip 0.85358pt

(with
s\!=\!\pm
) are defined in
Eq. (
40
).

In Eq.(
115
), the time-dependent factors

(\tau_{2}\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}
and
(\tau_{3}\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\nu}}

associated with the two soft internal lines have already been included
in the time integrals of the left and right subdiagrams. Hence, they do not appear in the bubble loop contribution. Because the Gamma-function factors contained in the nonlocal parts
are independent of the internal momenta
\mathbf{p}_{23}
and

\mathbf{p}_{31}
, we factorize the loop integral as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),abc\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}=\frac{\,\delta^{{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\sigma_{0\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\,}{4}\left\{\Gamma\right\}_{(d),abc}\!\!\int\!\!\frac{\,\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{q}\,}{\,(2\pi)^{3}\,}(2\mathbf{q})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}|2(\mathbf{q}\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{s})|^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}\,,

(116)

where
\left\{\Gamma\right\}_{(d),abc}
denotes the Gamma-function
from the two nonlocal soft propagators. This is precisely the form quoted
in Eq.(
98
) in Section
3.2.1
.

As shown in Appendix
C.4
,
the loop integral can be evaluated directly and we derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),abc\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}=\frac{\,\delta^{{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\sigma_{0\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\,}{4}\left\{\Gamma\right\}_{(d),abc}\frac{\,2^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}}k_{s}^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}}\,}{(4\pi)^{3/2}}\frac{\,\Gamma\big(\!-\!\frac{3}{2}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}\big)\big[\Gamma\big(\frac{3}{2}\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\big)\big]^{2}\,}{\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu})\big]^{2}\Gamma(3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})}\,.

(117)

We note that the above result contains a factor

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\tilde{\nu})\equiv\frac{~\Gamma\big(\hskip-1.42262pt\!-\!\frac{3}{2}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}\big)\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\Gamma\big(\frac{3}{2}\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\big)\big]^{\hskip-0.85358pt2}\,}{\big[\Gamma(\hskip-0.85358pt-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu})\big]^{\hskip-0.85358pt2}\Gamma(3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})}\,,

(118)

which arises from the loop-momentum integral. After removing the phase,
its large-
\tilde{\nu}
behavior scales as

|\mathcal{C}(\tilde{\nu})|\!\simeq\!2^{-9/2}\tilde{\nu}^{-3/2}
. For the sample input
\tilde{\nu}\!=\!60
[or
\tilde{\nu}\!=\!30\hskip 0.85358pt
],
this corresponds to a suppression factor

|\mathcal{C}(\tilde{\nu})|\!\simeq\!10^{-4}

[or
|\mathcal{C}(\tilde{\nu})|\!\simeq\!3\!\times\!10^{-4}\hskip 0.85358pt
]. Hence, despite that this factor is not apparent
from simple estimates in the previous works,
it does generate a large numerical factor to substantially
affect on the size of the loop contribution.

For the cases with
d\!=\!a\!=\!b\!=\!c
, the remaining Gamma function is given by

\displaystyle\left\{\Gamma\right\}_{(d),abc}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,\sum_{s}\!\!\Big[v_{s}(\tau_{2},\mathbf{p}_{23})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{23})\Big]_{\text{(NLoc)},d}\,}{(4\hskip 0.85358ptp_{23}^{2}\tau_{2}\tau_{3})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}}\frac{\,\sum_{s}\!\!\Big[u_{s}(\tau_{3},\mathbf{p}_{31})u_{s}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1},\mathbf{p}_{31})\Big]_{\text{(NLoc)},d}\,}{(4\hskip 0.85358ptp_{31}^{2}\tau_{3}\tau_{1})^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,e^{-2\pi\tilde{\lambda}}(1\!+\!e^{2\pi\tilde{\lambda}})\,\tilde{M}_{\!R}\left(e^{2\pi\tilde{\lambda}}\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{2}\!+\!\tilde{\lambda}^{2}\!-\!\tilde{\nu}^{2}\right)\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\big]^{4}\,}{\Gamma\!\big(\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\big)\Gamma\!\big(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\big)\big[\Gamma\!\big(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\big)\!\big]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{3}\sinh(2\pi\tilde{\lambda})\big[\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\big]^{4}}{\,\Gamma\!\left(\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\Gamma\!\left(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\big[\Gamma\!\left(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\!\big]^{2}\,}\,,

(119)

where in the last line we have used
\tilde{\nu}^{2}=\tilde{\lambda}^{2}\!+\!\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Substituting this expression back to Eq. (
117
),
we derive the bubble contribution as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.04996pt\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),abc\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}}\sigma_{0\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\,\frac{\,2^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}-3}\,}{\pi}\,k_{s}^{3+\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}}

(120)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\,\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{3}(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})^{3}\Gamma(-2\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\Gamma(-3\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu})\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\sinh(2\pi\tilde{\lambda})\tanh(d\pi\tilde{\nu})\,}{\Gamma\!\left(\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\Gamma\!\left(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\big[\Gamma\!\left(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\!\big]^{2}}\,.

The bubble contribution depends only on the types of the two soft propagators in the loop. Thus we can readily verify that the bubble term of diagram-(8) in Fig.
2
is identical to that of diagram-(7). More generally, we derive the following relations
for the bubble contributions from the different diagrams:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{(d),abc}=\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{(d),abc}\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\mathcal{B}^{(3)}_{(d),abc}=\mathcal{B}^{(4)}_{(d),abc}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(121)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(5)}_{(d),abc}=\mathcal{B}^{(6)}_{(d),abc}\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\mathcal{B}^{(7)}_{(d),abc}=\mathcal{B}^{(8)}_{(d),abc}\hskip 0.85358pt.

3.3
Full Result of the Three-Point Correlator

In this subsection, we are ready to derive the full result of the nonlocal contribution
to the three-point correlator from summing up all diagrams of Fig.
2
. For each given diagram-
(j)
of Fig.
2
, the nonlocal contribution
takes the following form:

\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow 0}\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(j)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\,\simeq\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c=\pm\\
d=\pm\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!a\hskip 0.85358ptb\hskip 0.85358ptc\,\mathcal{T}_{(d),ab}^{(\mathsf{L},j)}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{B}^{(j)}_{(d),abc}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{s})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{(d),c}^{(\mathsf{R},j)}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(122)

As discussed above, the dominant contribution comes from the cases with

d\!=\!a\!=\!b\!=\!c
. Thus, the above formula reduces to

\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow 0}\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(j)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\,\simeq\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=d=\pm\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!d\,\mathcal{T}_{(d),aa}^{(\mathsf{L},j)}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{B}^{(j)}_{(d),aaa}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{s})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{(d),a}^{(\mathsf{R},j)}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(123)

Then, we present the results for diagrams (7) and (8). Since these two diagrams share the same bubble contribution and the same right subdiagram,
the corresponding three-point correlator can be expressed as follows:

\hskip-14.22636pt\lim_{k_{s}\hskip-0.85358pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(7)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\!+\!\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(8)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\right]\simeq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=d=\pm\end{subarray}}\!\!\!d\!\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{(d),aa}^{(\mathsf{L},7)}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1})\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\!\mathcal{T}_{(d),aa}^{(\mathsf{L},8)}\!(\mathbf{k}_{1})\hskip-0.85358pt\right]\!\mathcal{B}^{(7)}_{(d),aaa}\!(\mathbf{k}_{s})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{(d),a}^{(\mathsf{R},7)}\hskip-1.42262pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(124)

For a three-point correlator
k_{s}\!=\!k_{3}
, we can compute the following product of
the bubble term and the right subdiagram
\mathcal{BR}^{(7)\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),a\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})
:

\mathcal{BR}^{(7)\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),a\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})\,\equiv\,\mathcal{B}^{(7)\hskip 8.04996pt\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),aaa\,\,\,\,{\dot{\gamma}}\gamma{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{s})\,\mathcal{T}_{(d),a}^{(\mathsf{R},7){\dot{\gamma}}\gamma}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})\,.

(125)

Substituting Eqs.(
110
) and (
120
) (with
d=a=b=c
)
into the above formula, we derive the product as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{BR}^{(7)\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),a\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\,-\mathrm{i}\,}{\,\Lambda\,}\frac{\,2^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}-4}e^{\pi\tilde{\nu}}(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})^{3}\Gamma(-2\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu})\Gamma(-3\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu})\,}{\Gamma\!\left(\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\Gamma\!\left(1\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\big[\Gamma\!\left(1\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\lambda}\!-\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358ptd\tilde{\nu}\right)\!\big]^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\sinh(2\pi\tilde{\lambda})\mathrm{csch}(2d\pi\tilde{\nu})\tanh(d\pi\tilde{\nu})k_{3}^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2d\tilde{\nu}}\tilde{M}_{\!R}^{3}\mathcal{A}^{\hskip 8.04996pt{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d)\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\alpha}}}\,,

(126)

where
\mathcal{A}^{\hskip 8.19447pt{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d)\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}
is a

2\!\times\!2
matrix and depends on the choice of
d\hskip 0.85358pt
,

\mathcal{A}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(+)\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}=\begin{pmatrix}-1&&0\\[2.84526pt]
0&&3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}\end{pmatrix}^{\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathcal{A}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(-)\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}=\begin{pmatrix}3\!+\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt4d\tilde{\nu}&&0\\[2.84526pt]
0&&-1\end{pmatrix}^{\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}_{\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}.

(127)

Then, the nonlocal CC signal of the three-point correlator from diagram-(7) and diagram-(8)
takes the following form:

\hskip-19.91692pt\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt0}\left[\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(7)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\!+\!\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(8)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\right]\simeq\!\sum_{a=d=\pm}\!\!\!d\!\left[\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\,{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),aa\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1})\!+\!\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8)\,{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),aa\hskip 8.04996pt\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1})\right]\!\mathcal{BR}^{(7)\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),a\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{3})\,,

(128)

where the term
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},7)\,{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),aa\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1})

is given by Eqs.(
3.2.2
) and (
3.2.2
), the term
\mathcal{T}^{(\mathsf{L},8)\,{\dot{\alpha}}}_{(d),aa\hskip 8.19447pt\!\!\!\!\!{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathbf{k}_{1})

is given by Eqs.(
106a
) and (
106b
),
and the term
\mathcal{BR}^{(7)\,\,\,\,\,{\dot{\beta}}}_{(d),a\,\,\,{\dot{\alpha}}}
is given by Eq.(
3.3
).

Figure 4
:
Contributions of diagrams (7)-(8) of Fig.
2

to the three-point correlator as a function of right-handed neutrino mass
M_{\!R}
for a set of sample
\Lambda
values. The plot (a) shows the contribution of the sum of the left subdiagrams,
the plot (b) depicts the contribution of the central bubble subdiagram, and
the plot (c) display the leading contribution of the right subdiagram. For comparison, the dashed curves of plot (a) also show the results as derived
by using the saddle point approximation
23
;
37
;
8
.

Finally, the nonlocal CC signal of the full three-point correlator is given
by the following sum of the eight diagrams of Fig.
2
:

\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt0}\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\,=\,\lim_{k_{s}\!\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt0}\sum_{j=1}^{8}\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(j)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\,.

(129)

The calculations for the remaining six diagrams are similar and we do not list
their explicit expressions here. Moreover, as proved in Appendix
C.1
, we have the following
conjugate relations between two diagrams:

\displaystyle\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(1)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}=\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(2)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]^{*},\hskip 34.1433pt\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(3)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}=\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(4)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]^{*},

(130)

\displaystyle\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(5)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}=\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(7)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]^{*},\hskip 34.1433pt\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(6)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}=\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\langle\delta\phi^{3}\rangle^{\prime(8)}_{\text{(NLoc)}}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]^{*}.

In the above analysis, we have chosen the small momentum
\mathbf{k}_{3}

to be on the
z
-axis and the large momentum
\mathbf{k}_{1}
to be approximately
aligned with the
z
-axis. For a more general configuration with
\mathbf{k}_{1}
having an angle
\theta

relative to the
z
-axis, we find that the total nonlocal cosmological collider signal
of the three-point correlator is independent of
\theta
. Hence the above analysis
applies to the general case.

In Fig.
4
, we present the
contributions of diagrams (7)-(8) of Fig.
2

to the three-point correlator as a function of right-handed neutrino mass parameter
M_{\!R}
for a set of sample values of the UV cutoff,

\Lambda\!=\!(60,\,80,\,100,\,120)H_{\mathrm{inf}}
[corresponding to

\lambda\!\simeq\!(60,\,45,\,36,\,30)H_{\mathrm{inf}}\hskip 0.85358pt
] which are shown respectively
by the (red, pink, blue, green) curves.

The left subdiagram shows the strongest dependence on both the mass parameter

M_{\!R}
and the chemical potential
\lambda\hskip 0.85358pt
. Its magnitude is significantly enhanced for larger chemical potential,
and for each benchmark choice it reaches its largest value around the intermediate-mass region. By contrast, the left subdiagram is relatively insensitive to
M_{\!R}

and mainly provides an overall
\tilde{\lambda}
-dependent normalization. The bubble contribution also peaks around the region of

M_{\!R}\!\sim\!(3\!-\!5)H_{\mathrm{inf}}
,
but unlike the left and right subdiagrams,
it becomes larger for smaller chemical potential.

For the left subdiagram, we also show in Fig.
4
(a)
the results obtained from using the saddle-point approximation of
Refs.
23
;
37
;
8
. In the small-mass region, the saddle-point approximation overestimates
the left subdiagram by roughly a factor of
{O}(10)
,
whereas in the large-mass region the discrepancy can increase to

{O}(10\!-\!100)
. Since the total contribution to the CC signal is determined by the product
of the three parts, the enhancement of the left and right
subdiagrams dominates the final result as the chemical potential increases. Consequently, the full oscillatory signal becomes stronger
for larger chemical potential and is most significant around the
intermediate-mass-parameter region.

4
Amplitude and Shape of Cosmological Collider Signatures

In the previous section, we derived the nonlocal oscillatory contribution to the inflaton three-point correlator generated by the right-handed neutrino loop. In this section, we study its phenomenological application. First, we analyze the cosmological collider signal (including its magnitude)
generated by the full three-point inflaton correlator
as contributed by all the eight diagrams in Fig.
2
. Second, we compare our result with that of the previous work
23

and discuss the difference between our study and the previous one.

4.1
Complete Cosmological Collider Signatures

Our goal in this subsection is twofold. We first express the squeezed-limit
bispectrum in the conventional non-Gaussianity normalization and extract the corresponding oscillatory amplitude parameter
f_{\mathrm{NL}}^{\mathrm{CC}}
. Then, we analyze the dependence of the signal
on the heavy right-handed neutrino mass and the cutoff scale,
and display the characteristic oscillatory shape
of the cosmological collider signal.
We will focus on the nonlocal contribution as computed
in Section
3
,
which captures the clock signal associated with
the right-handed-neutrino propagation.

According to the convention of Planck collaboration
6
,
the bispectrum of the Bardeen potential
\Phi
is defined as follows:

\langle\Phi_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}\!\Phi_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}\!\Phi_{\mathbf{k}_{3}}\rangle^{\prime}=\frac{\,6A^{2}f_{\mathrm{NL}}\,}{\,(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3})^{2}\,}\,\mathcal{C}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\,,

(131)

where
f_{\mathrm{NL}}
characterizes the magnitude of the primordial
non-Gaussianity, and
\mathcal{C}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
denotes the corresponding
shape template. The normalization constant
A
is defined through
the power spectrum of

\Phi
,
P_{\Phi}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\hskip-0.85358pt\langle\Phi_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}\Phi_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}\rangle^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\!{A}/{k^{3}}\,
,
where
k\!=\!|\mathbf{k}_{1}|\hskip 0.85358pt
. On the superhorizon scales, the Bardeen potential
is connected to the comoving curvature perturbation (
\zeta
) by the relation

\Phi\!=\!\frac{\,3\,}{\,5\,}\zeta\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and thus
A\!=\!2\pi^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,3\,}{\,5\,}\!\right)^{\!2}\!P_{\zeta}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
where
P_{\zeta}\!=\!\frac{k^{3}}{\,2\pi^{2}\,}\langle\zeta_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}\zeta_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}\rangle^{\prime}\simeq 2\!\times\!10^{-9}

is the observed dimensionless scalar power spectrum.

For the oscillatory cosmological collider signal, the bispectrum of
\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta

may be written as

\langle\zeta_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}\zeta_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}\zeta_{\mathbf{k}_{3}}\rangle^{\prime}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358ptf_{\mathrm{NL}}\frac{~72\hskip 0.85358pt\pi^{4}P_{\zeta}^{2}~}{~5\hskip 0.85358ptk_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}k_{3}^{2}~}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\,.

(132)

In the above the function
\mathcal{C}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is
the oscillatory template and takes the following form in the squeezed limit,

\mathcal{C}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=e^{\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\varphi_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\left(\hskip-0.85358pt\!\frac{\,k_{1}\,}{\,k_{3}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\right)^{\!\!\mathrm{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\tilde{\nu}-2}\!\!+\mathrm{c.c.}=2\!\left(\hskip-0.85358pt\!\frac{\,k_{1}\,}{\,k_{3}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\right)^{\!\!\!-2}\!\cos\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left(\hskip-0.85358pt\!2\tilde{\nu}\ln\!\frac{\,k_{1}\,}{\,k_{3}\,}\!+\!\varphi_{0}\!\right)\!,

(133)

where
\varphi_{0}
is an overall phase. Then, we derive the corresponding oscillatory non-Gaussian amplitude
f_{\mathrm{NL}}^{\mathrm{CC}}
as follows:

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{NL}}^{\mathrm{CC}}
